                                                                                                            Dubai crude futures volume on TOCOM rises 9.6%
                                                                                                            MOM to four-month high

                                                                                                            Traded volume down 36.91% year over year

                                                                                                            Dubai cash spread narrows amid weak fundamental

                                                                                                                The volume of Dubai crude oil futures contracts traded on
                                                                                                            the Tokyo Commodity Exchange rose 9.6% month over month
                                                                                                            to 56,868 lots in October, marking a four-month high. Volume
                                                                                                            was last recorded higher at 62,210 lots in June 2026, the latest
                                                                                                            TOCOM data showed Nov. 5.

                                                                                                                The figure was 36.9% lower than the traded volume reported
                                                                                                            in October 2025, according to the data.

FACTBOX: US crude, LPG gain footprint in Asia
amid shipping and supply risks

Elevated freight has changed comparative economics

South Korea, Japan sharply boost US crude purchases

US LPG flows to India surge more than fourfold

    US crude and LPG flows to Asia are rising sharply as refiners
and consumers seek to reduce their exposure to disruptions
affecting Middle Eastern production and shipping routes,
particularly around the Strait of Hormuz.

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Gasoil Daily Market
Analysis                                                                                   The relative weakness in the market reflects sentiment- driven
                                                                                       movements rather than a change in fundamentals, say traders.

                                                                       O Bunkering secures 41,000 mt/year MGO supply
                                                                       deal with OQ RPI in Oman

                                                                       Agreement to supply 48 million liters/year MGO

                                                                       Direct sourcing to strengthen supply security, pricing

                                                                           Oman-based bunker fuel supplier O Bunkering LLC signed
                                                                       a direct supply agreement with OQ Refineries and Petroleum
                                                                       Industries Co. for 48 million liters of marine gasoil annually,
                                                                       equivalent to about 41,000 metric tons/year, the company said in
                                                                       a statement seen by Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, Nov. 5.

                                                                           "The direct sourcing arrangement is expected to strengthen
                                                                       O Bunkering's supply chain and improve supply security, while
                                                                       enhancing its pricing competitiveness in the Omani marine fuel
                                                                       market," a company representative told Platts Nov. 5.
